# Product to category audit

Source: `data/processed/sales_data_cleaned.csv` | 5,680 products | 25 categories | 37 distinct raw product group values | 218,037 baskets | Rs 218,214,456.88 revenue

Generated by `scripts/export_category_audit.py`. Read-only: no data and no mapping was modified. Revenue is `total_amount` (VAT inclusive), which reconciles to `TOTAL_REVENUE` in `config/metrics.py`.

Categories with fewer than 10 products: ELECTRICAL SUPPLIES (8).

## Summary

One row per category, 25 rows, sorted by product count descending. `pct_of_baskets` is the share of the 218,037 baskets containing at least one product from the category; these do not sum to 100 because a basket holds several categories.

### Keyword rules used

Matching is whole-word and case-insensitive. A substring test is not safe here: "toilet" contains "oil", which would flag every toilet cleaner in CLEANING SUPPLIES.

| keyword | expected in | flag suppressed when name also contains |
| --- | --- | --- |
| oil | COOKING OIL | hair, massage, masage, scalp, body, skin, aroma, lamp, batti |

Two suppression rules stop the sheet filling with flavour names. Without them "BUTTER COOKIES" in BISCUITS AND COOKIES is flagged as misfiled dairy, which it plainly is not. A plain keyword test produced 501 flags; these two rules bring it to 413.

1. **Anchor.** If the name also contains a keyword whose expected category *is* the product's current category, the product is anchored where it sits and every other keyword in that name is treated as a flavour or ingredient word. "BUTTER COOKIES" is anchored by "cookies".
2. **Qualifier.** Per keyword, words that neutralise it outright, listed in the third column above. "HAIR OIL" and "MASSAGE OIL" are not cooking oil in any category.

Deliberately excluded as too ambiguous to be evidence of anything: "cream" (ice cream / face cream / cream biscuit), "powder" (milk / spice / washing / talcum), "gold" and "fresh" (brand words), "mix", "bar", "roll".
